LANCASHIRE COUNTY COUNCIL. PRELIMINARY ANNOUNCEMENT. SCHOLARSHIPS AND EXHIBITIONS. The Lancashire County Council intends offering tor com- petition during the month of June next, Fourteen Scholar- ships in Science, Four Scholarships in Art, and Six Scholar- ships in Commercial Subjects, each of the value of £6O per year for three years; and Twenty Scholarships in Agricultural and Horticulture, each of the value of £2O per year for three years. Also One Hundred Exhibitions in Science. Twenty-five Ex- hibitions in Art, Twenty-rive Exhibitions in Commercial Su jects. and Twenty-five Exhibitions in Agriculture and Horticulture, each of the value of £lO, tenable for one year at Evening Classes. Full particulars as to subjects and Voce of examination will be published shortly, and may, on publication, be obtained from the Town Clerks, Clerks to Local Boards, , Secretaries of the various Technical Schools, and Science and Art Classes throughout the Administrative County, or from the Director of Technical Instruction, County Offices, , Preston. I J. A. BENNION, Director of Technical Instruction for the County Palatine of Lancaster. County Offices, Preston, Feb. 2nd, 1893.

IN PARLIAMENT. SESSION, 1893. • RUNCORN WATER BILL. PRSUANT to a requisition in writing of 25 owners of property and ratepayers in the Distriat of the Runcorn Improvement Commissioners, I do HEREBY GIVE NOTICE that a PUBLIC MEETING of the owners and ratepayers of the District of the Runcorn Improvement Commissioners will be held, pursuant to the provisions of an Act of the 35th and 36th Victoria cap. 91. entituled " An Act " to Authorise the application of funds of Municipal Corpora- tions and other governing bodies in certain eases "at BETHESDA LECTURE HALL, VICA R-STREET, RUNCORN On THURSDAY the 162 n day of FEBRUARY, 1893, At seven o'clock in the evening. For the purpose of considering certain resolutions passed by the said Improvement Commissioners on the 28th day of November. 1892, and if thought desirable, of passing a resolu- tion confirming the said resolutions of the said Improvement Commissioners, and authorising them to take such steps as they shall deem necessary for promoting a Bill in the ensu- ing Session of Parliament intituled " A Bill to authorise the Runcorn Improvement Commissioners to acquire the under- taking of the Runcorn, Weston, and Halton Waterworks Company, and to confer further powers on the Commis- sioners." Dated this 8 h day of February, 1893. Town Hall, Runcorn. FRANCIS WISWALL, Chairman. Runcorn Improvement Commissioners.

THE HOMESTEAD LABOUR RIOTS. A Pittsburg telegram says : The trial of Jack Clifford on a charge of murdering one of the Pinkerton detectives named Conrad, during the labour riots at Homestead last summer, was concluded on Wednesday. The jury returned a verdict of not guilty, and the prisoner was discharged. `4 HOME RULE ALL ROjIND," MR. LABOUCHERE'S ANTICIPATION. Mr. Labouchere was last night to have presided at a discussion at the National Liberal Club on "Home Rule all round." Writing to excuse himself. owing to a cold, be stated, " I think that Home Rule all round will soon be adopted by the Liberal party as a portion of its pro- gramme. The Imperial Parliament cannot, owing to limitations of time, adequately act in Imperial and local ratters. Home Rule all round means the direct Influence of the people on all matters appertaining to its interests, and therefore it is the outcome of clemodacy."

Society of Chemical Industry (Manchester Section). I have chemically and microscopically examined Messrs. Crosby and Sons' " Cremoid " Calf Meal. I find It to contain albumininous substances which are necessary to build up the tissues of the animal system. The Meal also contains a considerable amount of oil, with carbo-hydrates, which tend to the production of fat. Nearly one-h .It of the ash is made up of phosphate of lime (thiq, I need not say, is one of the chief constituents of bones.) From the examina- tion of this Meal, I consider it an admirable food for the fattening of calves, as the Meal contains all the elements for the building up of the flesh and bones of the animal body. LY MM. __-- CONGRMAT§ONAL TF,A PARTY.—The anal tea party in connection with the Congregational Church, Lymm, was hold in the schoolroom, on Tuesday eiening. Tea was partaken of by a good number at six o'cleck, and at the meeting which' was held afterwards several gen- tlemen from the village and district gave addresses. The church choir rendered several songs during the evening. CONCERT.—On Monday evening a concert was held in the Drill Hall, Lymm, in aid of the church band. The entertainment was very enjoyable, the following being the artistes engaged :—Messrs. Evans, Huntingdon, Roper, T. Warrington, and the Misses Grundy and Thomas. Messrs. W. and T. Ball gave a duet (cornet euphonium), and the ringers gave a performance on handbells. LOCAL BOARD. The monthly meeting of the Lymm Local Board was held on Tuesday evening, when there were present—Mr. Gregson (chairman), Messrs. J. Holt, S. Appleton, R. Whitelegge, J. Perrin, A. Mclntyre,fl. Harrison, arid F. Swallow ; Mr. T. J. Ridgway (law clerk), Mr. W. Mul- lard (clerk), Mr. J. Mort (surveymf), Mr. W. Severs (gas manager), and Mr. W. Rouse (fire engine depart- ment).—Mr. Mullard stated that a g-rsat nuisance had been caused by certain boys in the village, who made a disturbance nightly near the Technical Nhool, disturb- ing the scholars there at their studies. He had caught one boy and had written to several parents telling them to bring their boys to the Board meeting. The parents and boys were then brought into the Boardroom, and , Mr. Ridgway gave the lads a severe reprimand, stating that they were very lucky in not having been prosecuted. He understood that the Board would in future prosecute all offenders.—The Surveyor (Mr. Mort) stated that the number of patients Raw at the Newfield Hospital was four, all of whom were convalescent. There had been no fresh cases since the 12th of January.—The Gas Manager (Mr. Severs) reported that the consumption of gas during the past month had been 1,823,000 cubic feet, as against 1,673,000' cubic feet for the corresponding month of last year.—Mr. Holt said that Mr. Fairclough had spoken to him about putting a new lamp at the corner of his works, with the words "Millstone Inn" upon it, if the Board would allow him to do so.—lt was decided that Mr. Holt should see Mr. Fairelough; and consult with him about the matter, so that Mr. Fair- dough could attend at the next meeting of the Board with his application.

A LIVERPOOL-ROAD SCANDAL; --- A " RESPECTABLE " FEMALE TERROR.. -- FRIGHTENED NEIGHBOURS BARRICADE DOORS. Sophia Eggington, a married woman, of Liver- pool-road, was summoned at the Borough Court, yesterday, for using threats- towards Mrs. Esther Ashcroft, win, of Mr. Ashcroft, insurance agent, Liverpool-road.—Mr. A. Browne appeatred for the complainant. The defendant did not appear.—Mr. Browne said the case was one of the most deplor— able that had appeared in that court. The defen- dant was the wife of a very respectable man, who was earning his .£8 and .ElO per week. She lived next door to Mrs. Ashcroft, and for months she had been in the habit of drinking and then threatening the complainant. Her children had to be sent to a board- ing school, and on sovera I occasions the police had been called in to quell disturbances. Mr. Isherwood, the landlord of the house had latterly been compelled to give Mr. Eggingron notice to quit in consequence of the annoyance caused by his wife to the neighbours and also inconsequence of the damage he had done to the ' property. A day after the notice had been served the defendant commenced drinking. She renewed her threats to Mrs. Ashcroft, and said she would take her !He. She had thrown a dolly peg through Ashcroft's window, and they and the other neighbours were so frightened that they had to barricade their doors. The defendant was a good-looking but strong woman. Hers was a deplorable instance of the evils of drink. She was constantly intoxicated and treated this violent behaviour as a large joke. One of the nei gh- bours said to her the other day: "You will be summoned for this?' " Well,that don't matter. It'll only cost a few quids- -1 shall be out of court as soon as them, and I'll go home and play the deuce with them." That was her reply. Mr. Browne asked that a warrant be issued for the defendant's arrest. When she was brought before them he should press for the full penalty. A warrant was granted.
